Given that there is no substantial decrease in the tolerability of mirvetuximab soravtansine in
patients with mild hepatic impairment, no dose adjustment is recommended for this
population.
The effect of moderate or severe hepatic impairment on the PK and safety of mirvetuximab
soravtansine has not been assessed. However, based on the known pathology of patients with
ovarian cancer, hepatic involvement leading to impaired hepatic function may be commonly
anticipated with the target patient population. The current version of the label Section 8.7
suggests avoiding use of mirvetuximab soravtansine use in patients with moderate or severe HI,
which would limit the availability of the treatment to these patients. Therefore, a PMR will be
issued to collect additional information to determine appropriate and safe dosing of
mirvetuximab soravtansine in patients with moderate hepatic impairment.
Folate Receptor alpha (FRα) Expression:
Eligibility for studies IMGN853-0401, IMGN853-0403, and IMGN853-0417 required patients to
have specific tumor expression levels of folate receptor alpha (FRα) as detected by the Ventana
FOLR1 IHC assay. If a patient did not have archival material available for analysis, a fresh biopsy
was obtained for enrollment. The IMGN853-0403 trial required minimum FRα expression levels
of ≥50% of tumor cells with any FRα membrane staining visible at ≤10X microscope
magnification. Additionally, a stratification cutoff of ≥75% of tumor cells with any FRα
membrane staining visible at 10X magnification was also applied. Analyses of the IMGN853Version date: October 12, 2018

of strong CYP3A4 inhibitors, patients receiving concomitant strong CYP3A inhibitors should be
closely monitored for signs of toxicity.

Review Strategy
Data Sources:
The clinical and statistical review of mirvetuximab soravtansine is primarily based on the
protocol, protocol amendments, clinical study report, patient narratives, Study Data Tabulation
Model (SDTM) and Analysis Data Model (ADaM datasets, and case report forms from Study
0417 (SORAYA) submitted by the Applicant on March 28, 2022. The review also includes
information for the safety update, submitted on July 25, 2022.
Supportive evidence of efficacy comes from Study 0416 (MIRASOL), an ongoing, phase 3,
randomized trial. FDA received an update on ORR/DOR from Study 0416 from a third party
during the review cycle for this BLA. The Applicant was blinded to these data. Further details
are included in Section 8.1.3 below. FDA received and reviewed topline data only. No datasets
were included.
Supportive evidence of safety comes from Study 0403 (FORWARD 1) and Study 0401. FDA
reviewed the protocol, clinical study report, patient narratives, datasets, and case report forms
from these trials as part of the integrated assessment of safety. Further details are included in
Section 8.2 below.
Data Quality and Integrity:
Data submitted with this application were in SDTM and ADaM formats. For the most part, data
were of adequate quality with adequate coding of preferred terms to allow review. Data were
generally consistent with information found in the CRFs. During the review, it was noted that
there were issues with the following items in the ocular toxicity data for the safety population:
Intraocular pressure measurements were missing, visual acuity measurements were entered in
error. For further details, see Ophthalmology General Consult Review by Dr. Wiley Chambers
dated 7/8/22. FDA sent the Applicant requests for additional information or clarification of
existing information using USUBJID, and for the most part, these were addressed in a timely
fashion. In addition, FDA conducted clinical site inspections which are described in Section 4 of
this review. There were no significant regulatory violations identified during the inspections
and it was concluded that Study IMGN853-0417 was overall properly conducted.

Reference ID: 5076613

{Mirvetuximab soravtansine}

8 Statistical and Clinical and Evaluation
Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
Study 0417 (SORAYA)
Trial Design
Study 0417 was a phase 3, single arm trial evaluating the safety and efficacy of mirvetuximab
soravtansine in patients with platinum-resistant high-grade serous epithelial ovarian cancer,
primary peritoneal cancer, or fallopian tube cancer with tumors that expressed a high level of
FRα. Patients were appropriate for single-agent therapy as the next line of therapy in the
opinion of the investigator.
The trial schema is shown in Figure 7.
Figure 7: Study 0417 Trial Design

Source: Applicant AOM slides
Study Endpoints
Primary endpoint
• ORR as assessed by investigator(INV) according to RECIST v1.1
Key secondary endpoint
• DOR as assessed by investigator(INV)
Additional secondary endpoints
• TEAEs, laboratory test results, physical exam findings, vital signs
• CA-125 response determined using GCIG criteria defined in Appendix C
• PFS as assessed by investigator according to RECIST v1.1
• OS
• ORR, DOR, and PFS as assessed by blinded independent central review according to
RECIST v1.1

Version date: October 12, 2018
Reference ID: 5076613

93

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761310}
{Mirvetuximab soravtansine}

Key Eligibility Criteria
Inclusion criteria
•

Female patients ≥18 years of age

•

Diagnosis of high-grade serous epithelial ovarian cancer, primary peritoneal cancer, or
fallopian tube cancer

•

Platinum-resistant disease defined as:
o If patients have received only 1 line of platinum-based therapy, they must have
received at least 4 cycles of a platinum agent, must have experienced a CR/PR,
and must have progressed >3 months but ≤6 months after the last dose
o Patients who have received 2-3 lines of platinum-based therapy must have
progressed on or within 6 months after the date of the last dose of platinum.
Patients with platinum-refractory disease during front-line treatment are
excluded.

Reviewer Comment: Definitions for “platinum-resistant” and “platinum-refractory” disease
vary. However, we note that in some definitions, patients who experience PD after completing
1L therapy but <3 months after the last dose may be considered to have platinum-resistant
disease. Those patients were excluded from enrolling to Study 0417.
We also note that patients who experience PD while receiving 2L+ platinum-based therapy may
be considered to have platinum-refractory disease in some definitions. These patients were
permitted to enroll to Study 0417.
•

Radiographic progression on or after most recent line of therapy

•

Willing to provide archival tumor tissue or undergo a fresh biopsy with a low-risk,
medically routine procedure for immunohistochemistry (IHC) to confirm FRα positivity

•

Tumor must be positive for FRα expression as defined by the Ventana FOLR1 assay

•

At least 1 lesion that meets the definition of measurable disease by RECIST v1.1 (as
determined by the investigator)

Version date: October 12, 2018
Reference ID: 5076613

94

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761310}
{Mirvetuximab soravtansine}
•

Received at least 1 but no more than 3 prior systemic lines of therapy, including at least
1 line of therapy containing bevacizumab, and must be appropriate for single agent
therapy as next line of treatment. Notes:
o (Neo)adjuvant treatment is considered 1 line of treatment
o Maintenance therapy is considered part of the preceding line of treatment (not a
separate line of treatment)
o If patients switch therapies due to toxicity without progression of disease (PD),
both therapies will be considered as the same line of treatment
o Hormonal therapy will count as a separate line of therapy unless it was given as
maintenance treatment

•

Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group (ECOG) Performance Status (PS) of 0 or 1

•

Completed prior therapies in the following timeframes:
o Systemic anticancer therapy within 5 half-lives or 4 weeks (whichever is shorter)
prior to first dose of mirvetuximab soravtansine
o Focal radiation at least 2 weeks prior to first dose of mirvetuximab soravtansine

•

Completed any major surgery at least 4 weeks prior to first dose

•

Recovered all prior treatment-related toxicities to Grade ≤ 1 (except alopecia)

•

Adequate organ function defined as:
o Absolute neutrophil count (ANC) ≥ 1.5 x 109/L without granulocyte colony
stimulating factor (G-CSF) in prior 10 days, hemoglobin ≥ 9.9 g/dL without
packed RBC transfusion in prior 21 days, platelet count ≥ 100 x 109/L without
platelet transfusion in past 10 days.
o Serum creatinine ≤ 1.5 x the upper limit of normal (ULN), aspartate
aminotransferase (AST) and alanine aminotransferase (ALT) ≤ 3 x ULN, serum
total bilirubin ≤ 1.5 x ULN (or ≤ 3 x ULN for patients with diagnosis of Gilbert
syndrome), serum albumin ≥ 2 g/dL

Version date: October 12, 2018

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761310}
{Mirvetuximab soravtansine}
•

Women of child-bearing potential must have a negative pregnancy test within 4 days
prior to first dose and must agree to use highly effective contraceptive methods while
receiving mirvetuximab soravtansine and for at least 3 months after the last dose

Exclusion Criteria
•

Male patients

•

Patients with tumors with endometrioid, clear cell, mucinous, or sarcomatous histology,
or mixed tumors containing any of these histologies, or low-grade/borderline ovarian
tumor

•

Primary platinum-refractory disease defined as disease that did not respond to (CR or
PR) or progressed within 3 months of the last dose of first-line platinum-containing
chemotherapy

•

Prior wide-field radiotherapy affecting at least 20% of bone marrow

•

Patients with > Grade 1 peripheral neuropathy per Common Terminology Criteria for
Adverse Events (CTCAE) criteria

•

Patients with active or chronic corneal disorders, history of corneal transplantation or
active ocular conditions requiring ongoing treatment/monitoring (e.g., uncontrolled
glaucoma, wet age-related macular degeneration requiring intravitreal injections, active
diabetic retinopathy with macular edema, macular degeneration, papilledema, and/or
monocular vision)

•

Patients with concurrent serious illness or clinically relevant active infection, including
but not limited to the following: active hepatitis B or hepatitis C infection, human
immunodeficiency virus (HIV), active cytomegalovirus (CMV), or any other concurrent
infection requiring IV antibiotics within 2 weeks prior to the first dose of mirvetuximab
soravtansine

•

History of multiple sclerosis or other demyelinating disease or Lambert-Eaton syndrome

•

Clinically significant cardiac disease including, but not limited to any of the following:
myocardial infarction ≤ 6 months prior to first dose, unstable angina pectoris,
uncontrolled CHF (New York Heart Association (NYHA) > class II), uncontrolled ≥ Grade 3
hypertension, uncontrolled cardiac arrhythmias

In Vitro Diagnostic
Assessment of tumor tissue for FRα expression occurred using the Ventana FOLR1 assay. This
was an immunohistochemistry (IHC) assay which detects FRα in formalin-fixed, paraffin
embedded (FFPE) tissue specimens using the Ventana OptiView DAB IHC Detection Kit. Testing
with the assay will occur at a central laboratory. The scoring method for the assay was based on
PS2+ which refers to membrane staining intensity of 2 or greater. The pathologist assessed the
percentage of tumors cells with moderate (2) or strong (3) membrane staining. FRα positivity
was defined as ≥75% of cells with level 2 or level 3 membrane staining intensity. Refer to the
CDRH consult for more details regarding the in vitro diagnostic device.
Treatment Regimen
Patients received mirvetuximab soravtansine 6 mg/kg of adjusted ideal body weight (AIBW)
administered on Day 1 of every 3-week cycle. Patients continued treatment until PD,
unacceptable toxicity, withdrawal of consent, death, or if the trial was terminated.
All patients received premedication with 325-650 mg PO acetaminophen, 10 mg IV
dexamethasone, and 25-50 mg PO/IV diphenhydramine approximately 30 minutes prior to each

Version date: October 12, 2018
Reference ID: 5076613

97

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761310}
{Mirvetuximab soravtansine}
infusion. An anti-emetic medication was recommended before each dose of mirvetuximab
soravtansine.
For repeat cycles, patients must have had an ANC ≥ 1.5 x 109/L, platelet count ≥ 100 x 109/L,
ocular disorders must have been recovered to ≤ Grade 1 or baseline, pneumonitis must have
been recovered to ≤ Grade 1, and all other non-hematologic toxicities must have been ≤ Grade
2 or baseline.
Patients could not take folate supplements during the trial.
Ocular Plan
All patients were required to use corticosteroid eye drops as prescribed by the treating
physician unless the risk outweighed the benefit in the opinion of the ophthalmologist or
treatment physician. Patients self-administered 1% prednisolone drops 6 times daily on Days -1
to 4, and 4 times daily on Days 5 to 8 of each cycle. Alternate corticosteroid eye drops could
have been prescribed.
All patients were required to use lubricating artificial tears on a daily basis. Patients were told
to wait at least 15 minutes after using corticosteroid eye drops to use artificial tears.
Patients were advised to avoid contact lenses. Patients were told to use baby shampoo and a
soft washcloth to clean eyes and could have used warm compresses at bedtime to decrease
ocular inflammation. Patients were told to wear UVA/UVB sunglasses during the trial. Also refer
to dose modifications and study assessments.
Tumor Assessments
Tumor imaging assessments with computerized tomography (CT) or magnetic resonance
imaging (MRI) of chest, abdomen, and pelvis occurred every 6 weeks (±1 week) from cycle 1,
day 1 (C1D1) for the first 36 weeks, and then every 12 weeks (±3 weeks) thereafter until
progression of disease, death, new anticancer treatment, or withdrawal of consent. Patients
who discontinued treatment for reasons other than PD continued to receive tumor imaging
assessments until PD or start of new anticancer therapy. All patients who discontinued the
study drug were followed every 3 months (±1 month) for survival follow-up until death, lost to
follow-up, withdrawal of consent, or the end of the trial, whichever occurred first.
CA-125 assessments were also planned to occur at same intervals as imaging assessments.

Statistical Analysis Plan
The primary endpoint was investigator-assessed confirmed ORR which included a best response
of either CR or PR. Along with a point estimate, the 95% exact confidence interval (CI) was
estimated by the Clopper-Pearson method. ORR was based on the Efficacy Evaluable
population, which included all patients who had measurable lesions at baseline and received at
least one dose of mirvetuximab.
The trial was planned to enroll approximately 105 patients, yielding 90% power to detect a
difference in ORR of 12% (24% vs. 12%) using a 1-sided binomial test. The target significance
level was 0.025 (1-sided). The actual significance level of the test was 0.0242. These targets
assumed an ORR of 12% under the null hypothesis and 24% under the alternate hypothesis.
Initial DOR assessment was planned to occur when ORR was determined. A second DOR
analysis was also planned to occur 12 months after primary analysis of ORR.
Reviewer comment: FDA does not conduct formal testing of ORR endpoints. FDA assesses the
ORR point estimate and 95% CI in the context of other factors including DOR, safety and
tolerability, and other available therapies.

Protocol Amendments
The key changes in each protocol amendment are listed below:
Protocol Amendment 1 (December 18, 2019)
• Increased the number of enrolled patients from 100 to 110 patients, to have 105
evaluable patients rather than 91 evaluable patients.
• Added that patients who discontinued mirvetuximab soravtansine for reasons other
than PD prior to week 36 and who did not initiate other anticancer therapy would
continue receiving scans every 6 weeks until week 36 (rather than every 12 weeks until
week 36). Patients would continue to receive scans every 12 months thereafter.
• Revised the definition of the efficacy evaluable population as those who received at
least one dose of mirvetuximab soravtansine and have measurable lesions at baseline
per RECIST v1.1.
• Added an exploratory objective to evaluate tumor-based biomarkers predictive of
response to mirvetuximab soravtansine
Protocol Amendment 1.1 (June 18, 2020)
• Added a reference to the Investigator statement to ensure that the investigator had
read the Ventana Assay Protocol Plan
Protocol Amendment 1.2 (July 22, 2020)
• Added recommendation to perform monthly pregnancy tests in women of childbearing
potential for 3 months following last dose of IP
• Revised list of contraceptive methods to include those considered highly effective
according to the Clinical Trials Facilitation and Coordination Group (CTFG) guidelines
Protocol Amendment 2 (August 28, 2020)
• Updated inclusion criterion regarding prior lines of therapy to state that patients who
had only received 1 prior line of therapy must have received at least 4 cycles and
progressed in >3 months but ≤ 6 months. Patients who had received 2-3 prior lines
could have progressed on or within 6 months following last dose of platinum. (Prior to
this update, all patients, regardless of prior lines of therapy, must have received at least
4 cycles of treatment.)
• Added an explicit statement that patients who had disease that was platinum-refractory
during 1L treatment were excluded.
• Stated that if ocular symptoms last >28 patients, patients may resume mirvetuximab
soravtansine with or without dose reduction if they experienced clinical benefit and if
agreed upon by Sponsor and Investigator.

Study Results
Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
The Applicant conducted Study 0417 in accordance with principles outlined in the International
Conference on Harmonization (ICH) Guideline for GCP. Safety monitoring was consistent with
the ICH-GCP E6 (R2) guidelines. The Applicant has a GCP audit program to ensure compliance
with applicable regulations including ICH E6 R2, and federal, state, and local regulations. The
Applicant conducted independent site audits. The Applicant’s GCP compliance program
determined that Study 0417 was conducted in compliance with GCP and that the trial data are
reliable.
Financial Disclosure
Financial disclosure information is summarized in Section 19.2. It is unlikely that the disclosable
financial interests affected the conduct of Study 0417.

Reviewer comment:
The Surveillance, Epidemiology, and End Results (SEER) database provides data regarding the
relative incidence of ovarian cancer cases by race. The relative incidence of ovarian cancer per
100,000 women is 13.1 in White patients, 10.2 in American Indian patients, 9.0 in Black
patients, and 8.4 in Asian/Pacific Islander patients. Regarding ethnicity, the relative incidence of
ovarian cancer cases per 100,000 women is 9.4 for Hispanic patients and 13.0 for patients who
are not Hispanic. Although there is a lower incidence of ovarian cancer among Black patients,
they may experience worse outcomes. One study showed that Black patients experienced the
lowest 5-year survival compared to White patients, Hispanic patients, and Asian patients at
each stage of disease (Goodman 2003).
It is challenging to determine the distribution by race or ethnicity of patients in the U.S. with
advanced ovarian cancer. However, the FDA review team does not consider the Study 0417 trial
population to be representative of a U.S. based population with ovarian cancer with respect to
race and ethnicity. In terms of race, Study 0417 enrolled 96% patients who were White, 2% of
patients who were Asian, and 2% of patients who were “Other” (further details unknown). In
terms of ethnicity, Study 0417 enrolled only 2% of patients who were Hispanic or Latino (Park
2017).
FDA issued a PMC regarding race and ethnicity. FDA recommended that the Applicant conduct
an integrated analysis including data from clinical trials and other data sources to further
characterize the safety and efficacy of mirvetuximab soravtansine in patients from racial and
ethnic minority groups. See Section 13 for more details.

Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use
The Applicant monitored drug accountability to ensure that mirvetuximab soravtansine was
only given to qualified personnel. Treatment compliance was monitored and captured in the
eCRF including data regarding the time of the dose, the actual dose administered, dose
modifications, and other details regarding mirvetuximab soravtansine administration. Of note,
ocular toxicity and infusion-related reactions (IRRs) are safety signals of concern in the
mirvetuximab soravtansine development program. Details regarding concomitant medication
administration for prophylaxis and management of these toxicities can be found in 8.2: Review
of Safety.
Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint
The primary efficacy results for INV-assessed ORR are shown in Table 26. Mirvetuximab
provided an ORR of 31.7% ([95% CI: 22.9, 41.6] by investigator) in participants with platinum
resistant, advanced, high-grade epithelial ovarian, primary peritoneal, or fallopian tube cancers
with high folate receptor-alpha expression.
The median DOR in the original BLA submission, based upon a data cutoff (DCO) date of
11/16/21, was 5.9 months (95% CI: 5.6, 7.7 months) with a range of 1.4+ to 9.8 months.
As part of the 120-Day Update, the Applicant included information regarding DOR. The DCO
date for the DOR update was 4/29/22 and included an additional 5 months of follow-up. With
longer follow-up, among the 33 patients in the INV Efficacy Evaluable population with a

{Mirvetuximab soravtansine}
confirmed CR or PR, the updated median DOR per RECIST was 6.9 months (95% CI: 5.6, 9.7).

Integrated Review of Effectiveness
Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials
The confirmatory, randomized trial, Study 0416 (MIRASOL, NCT04209855), was ongoing during
FDA’s review of the current application. During a Type B pre-BLA meeting held in January 2022
to discuss the topline results from Study 0417, FDA indicated that they would request ORR and
DOR data from the ongoing trial Study 0416 during review of the Study 0417 results, in order to
achieve an early indication as to the outcome of trial that would be used to confirm clinical
benefit, in the event of an accelerated approval based on Study 0417. During the current
review, FDA sent a formal request to the Applicant, requesting blinded ORR and DOR data from
both arms of the ongoing, randomized trial, Study 0416. To maintain trial integrity, the
Applicant used a third-party vendor to generate the requested results and put in place
procedures to prevent anyone from the company from accessing these results, so that the
Applicant would remain blinded.
Study 0416 is an ongoing, randomized, phase 3 clinical comparing mirvetuximab soravtansine
to investigator’s choice chemotherapy in patients with platinum-resistant, high-grade epithelial
ovarian, primary peritoneal, or fallopian tube cancer, whose tumors have a high level of FRα
expression. Patients should be appropriate for single agent chemotherapy to enroll to this trial,
can have received 1-3 prior lines of systemic therapy, and are permitted (but not required) to
have received bevacizumab. Patients are also permitted to have received prior PARP inhibitors.
Study 0416 is occurring in a similar treatment to Study 0417, except patients were required to
have received prior bevacizumab to enroll to SORAYA. Options for investigator’s choice
chemotherapy included paclitaxel, PLD or topotecan. The primary endpoint is PFS and
secondary endpoints include ORR, DOR, and OS.
The data cut-off date for the ORR and DOR data from Study 0416 was May 13, 2022. FDA only
reviewed topline results and did not receive datasets. The topline ORR and DOR results from
Study 0416 were supportive of the efficacy of mirvetuximab soravtansine in patients with FRαpositive, platinum-resistantant epithelial ovarian, fallopian tube, or primary peritoneal cancer
who have received 1-3 prior lines of systemic therapy.
Reviewer Comment: At the time of the ORR/DOR update, Study 0416 had enrolled 361 (84%)
patients out of the planned 430 patients.

{Mirvetuximab soravtansine}
Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness
FDA’s conclusions regarding efficacy are primarily based on results from Study 0417(SORAYA), a
phase 3, single arm trial evaluating the safety and efficacy of mirvetuximab soravtansine in
patients with FRα-positive, platinum-resistant, high-grade serous epithelial ovarian cancer,
primary peritoneal cancer, or fallopian tube cancer. In Study 0417, the ORR assessed by INV per
RECIST v1.1 was 31.7% (95% CI: 22.9, 41.6). Median DOR was 6.9 months (95% CI: 5.6, 9.7).
Results for ORR and DOR as assessed by BICR were generally consistent with results as assessed
by INV, with an overall concordance of 86% for ORR assessment between INV and BICR. ORR
results were also generally consistent across subgroups.
Patients with platinum-resistant ovarian cancer, particularly those who have received prior
bevacizumab, represent an area of unmet medical need. Treatment options are limited and
generally include single agent chemotherapy, with ORRs ranging from 6-27% in previous trials
(Table 2). DOR data are scant. An important caveat when interpreting these response rates is
that many of the trials with single agent chemotherapies were conducted before establishment
of a 1L treatment regimen containing a platinum-based doublet ± bevacizumab followed by
maintenance treatment with bevacizumab, niraparib, olaparib , or olaparib + bevacizumab as
standard of care for advanced ovarian cancer. The efficacy of the single agent chemotherapies
for treatment of platinum-resistant ovarian cancer may be less than seen in historical trials.
A better benchmark for available therapies in the current treatment era may be the
chemotherapy control arm from recent trials in platinum-resistant ovarian cancer. These trials
are shown in Table 2, and the ORR is 12-13% and median DOR is 4-7.4 months. In addition, not
all patients included in these trials had received prior bevacizumab, and many patients had
received only 1-2 prior lines of treatment. All patients enrolled to Study 0417 had received prior
bevacizumab, and 50% of patients had received 3 prior lines of treatment, representing a more
heavily pre-treated population. There are no available therapies specifically for patients with
FRα-positive platinum-resistant ovarian cancer.
To support the efficacy results from Study 0417, FDA requested blinded topline ORR/DOR data
from Study 0416, an ongoing randomized trial in a similar treatment setting to Study 0417.
These results were supportive of the efficacy of mirvetuximab soravtansine in patients with
FRα- positive platinum-resistant ovarian cancer.
Given the results from Study 0417, supported by additional data from Study 0416, and
considering the current therapeutic landscape for patients with platinum-resistant ovarian
cancer who have received 1-3 prior lines of therapy, including prior bevacizumab, FDA considers
an ORR of 31.7% (95% CI: 22.9, 41.6) and a median DOR of 6.9 months (95% CI: 5.6, 9.7) to
represent an improvement over available therapies. The review team determines that these
results are reasonably likely to predict clinical benefit for mirvetuximab soravtansine in patients
with FRα-positive, platinum-resistant ovarian cancer who have received 1-3 prior systemic
treatment regimens. Patients will be selected for treatment using VENTANA FOLR1 (FOLR1-2.1)
RxDx Assay. Confirmation of clinical benefit will be based on results from Study 0416, the
Version date: October 12, 2018
Reference ID: 5076613

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761310}
{Mirvetuximab soravtansine}
ongoing randomized clinical trial comparing mirvetuximab soravtansine to investigator’s choice
chemotherapy in patients with FRα-positive, platinum-resistant ovarian cancer.

Review of Safety
Safety Review Approach
Data:
The safety profile summarized in this application consists of data from 3 clinical trials of
mirvetuximab soravtansine including 464 patients. All patients received the recommended
starting dose of 6mg/kg based on AIBW of mirvetuximab soravtansine. The number of patients
in each of the safety analysis groups is described below.
The Study 0417 group includes 106 patients in the safety population, and this was the pivotal
trial submitted for this application. All patients had high-grade serous ovarian cancer. All
patients had received 1-3 prior lines of systemic treatment and 100% had received
bevacizumab. Tumor samples were assessed to have high FRα expression in all patients.
The pooled safety set included all epithelial ovarian cancer (EOC) patients who received
mirvetuximab soravtansine 6mg/kg AIBW Q3W on one of 3 studies, including Studies 0401,
0403, 0417. This included EOC patients (expansion cohorts 1, 3, and 5) in Study 0401 (N=113),
the Phase 3 portion of Study 0403 (N=245), and all patients in Study 0417 (N=106). Of note, 142
of the total n=464 patients had tumors with FRα high expression according to the criteria used
in Study 0417.
The primary evidence of safety is derived from the Study 0417 data, as the subjects represent a
more homogeneous patient population than the pooled data. Patients enrolled in Study 0417
had tumors with high FRα expression and received the same protocol-specified mitigation
measures and dose modification. Key features of Study 0417 compared to the pooled analysis
data sets from Study 0401, 0403 include:
• Study 0417 had implementation of the most up to date recommended dose
modifications and mitigation strategies for AEs. In particular, this included changes in
the ocular toxicity mitigation strategies from prophylactic corticosteroid eye drops for
10 days (Study 0401) to 8 days per cycle (Studies 0403, 0417).
• Study 0417 had a more homogeneous population based upon FRα High-expression
• The eligibility criteria for Study 0417 included limitations on the number of allowable
prior therapies (1 to 3 prior systemic lines of anticancer therapy, including mandatory
receipt of prior bevacizumab).
Reviewer comment: There were no obvious discrepancies identified between the analysis
datasets and the information provided in the Clinical Study Reports. The applicant’s
categorization of data and coding methods were appropriate. The preferred terms (PTs) listed in

AEs of Special Interest including peripheral neuropathy, ocular toxicities, and pneumonitis to

The size of the safety population is different from the size of the efficacy population for the
single arm trial 0417. The safety population on Study 0417 consisted of all patients who received
at least one dose of Mirvetuximab (n=106). The efficacy population included all patients who
had efficacy evaluable disease, as per INV assessment, and this led to exclusion of one patient
due to lack of measurable disease and one patient due to protocol violation. Therefore, the
efficacy population included n=104 patients.

The safety analysis for this BLA is primarily based on data from 106 safety-evaluable patients
who received at least 1 dose of study drug and who had at least one post-baseline safety
assessment Study 0417. But as noted, the Applicant also provided datasets for the two other
clinical studies, the randomized Study 0403 and a dose-escalation phase of the FIH study, Study
0401. The pooled safety cohort including 464 patients from studies 0401, 0403, and study 0417
is considered adequate for an assessment of the safety of mirvetuximab soravtansine.
Adequacy of Applicant’s Clinical Safety Assessments
Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
FDA review of the ocular toxicity data submitted by the Applicant revealed that there were
visual acuity values that appeared to have been entered into the database in error for study
0403. In addition the BCVA and IOP shift tables in the CSR for Study 0417 had missing data. The
Applicant resolved these issues through response to information request on July 18, 2022.
Reviewer’s Comments: Visual acuity and intraocular pressure data were originally not
considered to be interpretable because of a large number of missing values; additionally there
were limited assessments by fundoscopy, dilated slit lamp and evaluations of tear production.
This was noted via ophthalmology consultation with Dr. Wiley Chambers and is described in his
consult review. The subsequent response and submission of additional information by the
Applicant was also reviewed by Dr. Chambers and was considered acceptable to proceed with
safety assessment. Though not all of the missing data was obtained, the response was
considered adequate to proceed with safety review and did not change the overall safety
assessment for the application.
Categorization of Adverse Events
Safety and tolerability assessment was based on the frequency of deaths, AEs, serious adverse
events (SAEs), AEs leading to discontinuation, AEs leading to interruptions, select AEs, clinical
laboratory assessments (hematology, serum chemistry, liver, kidney and thyroid function tests),
and vital sign measurements. AEs were coded using the MedDRA version 20.0. AEs and
laboratory values were graded for severity using the NCI CTCAE version 4.0. Adverse events
were categorized by System Organ Class (SOC) and Preferred Term (PT). The Applicant prespecified adverse events of special interest (AESIs) based on Event of Clinical Interest Guidance.
For this application, AESIs comprised of pneumonitis, ocular toxicities, peripheral neuropathy,
and infusion-related reactions.

Deaths
There were 33 (31%) deaths in the overall safety population of 106 patients that received
mirvetuximab. The majority (25%) of deaths were due to disease progression. Three patients in
Study 0417 experienced AEs that led to death. The AEs leading to death were intestinal
obstruction (N=2), and respiratory failure (N=1).
A summary of deaths within 30 days of last study treatment is provided in Table 32. In Study
0417, 7 patients died within 30 days of study drug administration. Four of these deaths were
due to disease progression and 2 of these were attributed to adverse events, 1 patient
experienced pneumonitis and died of respiratory failure and one patient died of a small bowel
obstruction. There was 1 patient who developed a colonic obstruction and was hospitalized and
subsequently died, but no imaging was conducted to confirm the cause.
Narrative summaries for each TEAE leading to death are provided in the respective CSRs.

the ovary complicated by diffuse alveolar damage on a background of idiopathic pulmonary
fibrosis with recent clinical bronchopneumonia.
(b) (6)

Reviewer comment: FDA acknowledged that the patient had advanced ovarian cancer, but
considered the cause of death to be most likely due to respiratory failure in the setting of
pneumonitis caused by mirvetuximab. The Applicant agreed that because pneumonitis was a
plausible contributor to the patient’s respiratory failure and death, this patient is described in
product labeling as having had an adverse event (pneumonitis) leading to death.

Electrocardiograms (ECGs)
All patients enrolled on Study 0417 underwent a 12-lead ECG prior to the first dose of
mirvetuximab; subsequent ECGS on study were not scheduled, but could have been obtained
by investigators as needed. Five patients experienced TEAEs that were classified as arrythmias
(2 with sinus tachycardia, 1 supraventicular arrhythmia, 1 atrial fibrillation and 1 ECG
repolarization abnormality). These events were assessed as not likely related to mirvetuximab
therapy. It was determined mirvetuximab did not cause meaningful changes in ECG findings
over time.
QT
QT/IRT consultation was requested for review of this initial BLA application. The QT team
previously reviewed QT data collected during Study IMGN853-0401 (Study 0401), which was
conducted under IND 111915. An additional QT study was not performed on Study 0417
The QT data from Study 0401 revealed no large changes in mean QTc intervals (>20 msec) at
the recommended dose of mirvetuximab soravtansine (6 mg/kg AIBW every 3 weeks).
Likewise, no increases in QTcF of > 500 msec were observed in patients treated with
mirvetuximab soravtansine. Central tendency analysis of the QTcF interval changes from
baseline showed that the highest mean (upper bound of the 2-sided 90% CI) for QTcF was 8.1
(4.1, 13.0) msec. This level of change was observed at cycle 3/Day 1 in the Expansion cohort 3
on that study. As a result of this assessment, Section 12 of product labeling has included
language to convey that, at the approved dose, mirvetuximab does not cause a large mean
increase (> 10 msec) in the QTc interval.
Reviewer comment: See also QT consult by Drs. Kitabi, Ruiz, and Stockbridge, dated 6/30/22.

Immunogenicity

Version date: October 12, 2018
Reference ID: 5076613

135

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761310}
{Mirvetuximab soravtansine}
Potential for immunogenicity against mirvetuximab was assessed by testing of blood samples
for the presence of anti-drug antibodies (ADA) at timepoints including C1D1, C2D1, C4D1, end
of therapy and at 30-day follow up. These assessments were performed on 3 separate studies,
including 0417, 0401, and 0403. The analysis included data from 423 patients with ovarian
cancer treated across the studies. With a median duration of treatment of 4.3 months, 55/423
(13%) of ovarian cancer patients treated at the 6 mg/kg mirvetuximab dose had at least 1 postbaseline positive sample for ADA. Of these, 28/423 (7%) developed treatment-emergent ADA
and 3/423 (0.7%) developed treatment enhanced ADA. Neutralizing antibodies were detected
in 24/423 (6%) of patients.
Reviewer comment: The conclusion about the immunogenicity data is that the overall
occurrence of anti-mirvetuximab antibodies across the three clinical studies was relatively low.
It is not known what effect these antibodies had or may have on the PK, efficacy, or safety of
mirvetuximab, therefore, at this time the impact of ADA on the PK, safety, and efficacy of
mirvetuximab is not fully characterized. Pertinent information on immunogenicity was included
in Section 12 of product labeling.
Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues

Ocular Adverse Events/Toxicity
Ocular AEs are an adverse event of special interest with mirvetuximab. FDA conducted an
independent assessment of the ocular toxicity, focusing mainly on Study 0417, but also
considering data from Studies 0401 and 0403 (FORWARD1). See also Ophthalmology Consult
from Dr. Wiley Chambers dated 7/8/22.
Terms included in conducting the analysis of ocular toxicities included conjunctival disorders,
corneal disorders, lacrimal disorders (dry eye), eye disorders (broad SOC), ocular infections,
optic nerve disorders, periorbital and eyelid disorders, scleral disorders, and retinal disorders.
Ocular monitoring on Study 0417 included the use of ocular examinations when symptoms
were reported according to CTCAE criteria. Prophylactic use of corticosteroids and lubricating
tears were practices that were a part of the study protocol and were implemented for all
patients on study.
An overview of the ocular toxicities on Study 0417 is shown in Table 39.
The majority of patients (59%) had ocular TEAEs on Study 0417. The most common ocular
adverse events were visual impairment (54%), keratopathy (37%), and dry eye (26%). The
majority of ocular adverse events were of grade 1-2 in severity, but 8% of patients on Study
0417 experienced grade 3-4 visual impairment and 9% of patients experienced grade 3-4

adverse events on Study 0417 did not have resolution of their ocular AEs at the last follow-up.

Ocular analysis in safety database
An analysis of ocular toxicities in the pooled population of 464 patients with ovarian cancer
treated on Studies 0417, 0403, and 0401 was also conducted and is shown in Table 40. Overall,
the incidence of severe ocular AEs was similar between Study 0417 and in the pooled
population, with 61% of patients treated with mirvetuximab experiencing an ocular adverse
event. There were slight differences in the frequency of dose interruptions and reductions in
the pooled databased, as compared to Study 0417 alone, however the frequency of treatment
discontinuations due to ocular AEs was low. According to FDA analysis, 30% of patients in the
safety database had ocular AEs that were listed as not recovered by the end of follow-up.

Reviewer comment:
It is notable that the ocular monitoring, management, and risk mitigation strategy for patients
receiving mirvetuximab evolved over the course of the development program. On the dose
escalation study, 0401, there was minimal monitoring and patients were only assessed based
upon reports of ocular AEs. As it became more apparent that ocular toxicities were a key
adverse event in patients receiving mirvetuximab, the Applicant implemented scheduled
monitoring and prophylactic measures in an attempt to improve the ocular tolerability.
FDA assessed the use of CTCAE grading of severity of ocular toxicities, as well as the
interventions for ocular AEs, based on patient-reported symptoms, rather than routine
examinations. One difficulty in conducting the ocular safety review for the mirvetuximab
database relates to the use of the CTCAE grading system for ocular adverse events, which relies
on symptom-based reporting of AEs. The reason this system is not ideal for ocular adverse
events is because there are certain ocular adverse events, such as corneal ulcers, that can be
present without symptoms until the event has become severe (Grade 3, which correlates with
corneal perforation). In this situation, the adverse event will not be detected until the toxicity
has become significant. Additionally, certain ocular adverse events, such as cataracts, do not
typically resolve on their own, even after discontinuation of the offending agent.
Discussions took place between the Applicant and FDA, regarding the issue of the reporting of
partial and complete resolution of ocular toxicities. It was ultimately agreed upon between FDA
and the Applicant that out of the safety database, (n=464 patients), 61% experienced ocular
adverse events. Of these patients, 49% subsequently had resolution of their ocular AEs and 39%
had partial resolution, which was defined as improvement by one or more grades from worst
severity grade. It is notable that although many patients reported having at least partial
improvement of ocular toxicities, it could be argued that any ocular toxicity, regardless of grade,
may affect quality of life, including that even Grade 2 toxicity may involve some vision loss.
Given the severity and prevalence of the ocular toxicities associated with mirvetuximab therapy,
the ability of the ocular toxicities to significantly affect patient functioning and quality of life,

Version date: October 12, 2018
Reference ID: 5076613

138

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761310}
{Mirvetuximab soravtansine}
and the need for specialized monitoring and mitigation measures, ocular toxicity was included in
product labeling as a Boxed Warning for prescribers.
In addition, it was determined by the review team that a post-marketing requirement (PMR)
was needed, mandating that the Applicant take steps to identify additional measures which
may improve the ocular tolerability for patients receiving mirvetuximab. The PMR mandates
that the Applicant conduct or amend an ongoing clinical trial to incorporate a prospectively
specified ocular monitoring approach. The monitoring will need to employ scheduled ocular
assessments of patients throughout the trial, rather than utilizing symptom-based ocular
examinations only. In this trial, the Applicant will also need to investigate additional risk
mitigation strategies, such as supportive care prophylaxis. It was advised, and is expected, that
the Applicant will meet with FDA in the future to discuss proposed trial designs and mitigation
plans as part of an approach to fulfill the post-marketing requirement.
Peripheral neuropathy
Peripheral neuropathy was identified as another adverse event of special interest for
mirvetuximab, given that the payload, DM4, is a tubulin-directed compound. Patients with >
Grade 1 peripheral neuropathy at baseline were excluded from Study 0417, which is relevant to
the study population with ovarian cancer, given that these are patients who have received prior
neurotoxic chemotherapies including taxanes and platinum.
On Study 0417 (n=106), 28% of patients reported all-grade adverse events of peripheral
neuropathy while on study. The analysis of these events involved pooling of terms including
neurotoxicity, peripheral sensory and motor neuropathy, hypoesthesia, polyneuropathy,
neuralgia, and oral hypoesthesia. The incidence of severe peripheral neuropathy on Study 0417
was 2%, including two patients experiencing Grade 3 neuropathy, one of whom discontinued
treatment with mirvetuximab due to severe peripheral neuropathy.
The narratives for the two patients experiencing Grade 3 neuropathy on Study 0417 are
discussed as follows:
Patient 0417: Event reported as grade 3 peripheral sensory and motor neuropathy /
sciatica; resulted in treatment discontinuation. This patient experienced Grade 2 motor
neuropathy and Grade 3 sensory neuropathy on Day 144 of treatment and did have a past
medical history of prior platinum chemotherapy and diabetes mellitus. However, the patient did
not have baseline neuropathy reported and this AE was thought to be related to the study drug
and led to discontinuation of the drug. She subsequently discontinued treatment for progression
(b) (6)
of disease. The end of treatment visit was on
and the AEs of Grade 2 Peripheral
motor neuropathy and Grade 3 Peripheral sensory neuropathy were not resolved and were
assessed by the investigator as probably related to mirvetuximab soravtansine. FDA agrees with
the attribution to study drug.
(b) (6)

Reference ID: 5076613

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761310}
{Mirvetuximab soravtansine}
Patient 0417: Event of peripheral motor neuropathy. This patient experienced Grade 3
neuropathy after 7 cycles of mirvetuximab. The patient had received chemotherapy prior to
enrollment in Study 0417 but was not noted to have baseline neuropathy. The peripheral
neuropathy was not resolved at the time of the patient’s last assessment and she discontinued
treatment one month later due to progression of disease.
(b) (6)

In the larger safety database of 464 patients with ovarian cancer who received mirvetuximab
on clinical trials, peripheral neuropathy was reported in 167 patients (36%). This included 9
patients with G3 peripheral neuropathy (2%) and 2 patients (0.4%) who discontinued
mirvetuximab due to peripheral neuropathy. The median time to onset of peripheral
neuropathy symptoms was 1.3 months (range 0.03 to 29.1 months). Of patients who
experienced peripheral neuropathy during therapy with mirvetuximab, 28% had complete
resolution and 13% had partial improvement in neuropathy symptoms. Partial improvement
was defined as a decrease in severity by one or more grades from the worst grade experienced.
This assessment also indicates that 98 patients (59%) in the mirvetuximab database who
experienced peripheral neuropathy while receiving mirvetuximab did not have resolution
(partial or complete) of these events, though majority of events were G1-2 in severity.
Reviewer comment: It is notable that the incidence of peripheral neuropathy in the larger
safety base was higher than on the Study 0417 (36% vs. 28%), the reason for this is unclear,
however, the majority of events throughout were Grade 1 or Grade 2 in severity. FDA noted that
eligibility criteria in Study 0417 permitted enrollment of patients with baseline Grade 1
neuropathy. Review of the data revealed that 15 patients on the Study 0417 had Grade 1
neuropathy at study baseline. Of the patients who had adverse events of peripheral
neuropathy, 7 of them had neuropathy at baseline. Neither of the 2 patients who experienced
Grade 3 neuropathy on study had neuropathy at baseline, and their events were not completely
resolved at data cutoff. While peripheral neuropathy is also a risk with certain chemotherapy
agents and was seen at higher rates with paclitaxel in the randomized Study 0403, FDA
disagrees with the Applicant’s claim that the rates of neuropathy seen with Mirvetuximab are
an “improvement over all chemotherapy agents and regimens available to patients in the
second-line setting”, as this cannot be adequately assessed in the absence of a randomized trial,
comparing the agents directly. Both the pooled analysis and the individual analysis of peripheral
neuropathy for Study 0417 supported the addition of peripheral neuropathy to the Warnings
and Precautions section of the prescribing information for Mirvetuximab.
Pneumonitis
Pneumonitis affected 8% of patients on Study 0417. Though there were no cases of Grade 3-4
pneumonitis events reported on Study 0417, there were four (n=4) Grade ≥3 events in the
overall safety population including 464 patients with ovarian cancer. There was one patient on
Study 0417 who died due to respiratory failure in the setting of pneumonitis. The narrative was
described previously in this review in Section 8.2.2 (discussion of death narratives).

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761310}
{Mirvetuximab soravtansine}
Reviewer comment: Pneumonitis was included in product labeling as a Warning and Precaution
in Section 5 with guidance for investigators to monitor for and manage with potential toxicity.
Hypersensitivity and Infusion-related reactions
Hypersensitivity and infusion-related reactions (IRRs) were designated by the Applicant as AEs
of Special Interest. The initial FDA review indicated the incidence of all-grade hypersensitivity
reactions to be 13% on Study 0417 and the incidence of all-grade infusion-related reactions to
be 5%. However, FDA found overlap with patients experiencing hypersensitivity and IRR AEs
and recommending grouping these events into one category. As a result, the reported
incidence of the pooled terms for all-grade hypersensitivity / infusion-related reactions was 9%
on Study 0417. There was one patient who experienced a Grade 3 infusion-related reaction
which was an SAE and resulted in permanent discontinuation of mirvetuximab, and the
narrative is described below.
Patient 0417- The event was reported to be a Grade 3 infusion-related reaction at Cycle
2 of treatment, which included hypotension and hypoxia. This resolved after 12 hours and was
attributed to mirvetuximab soravtansine treatment. The patient discontinued treatment
following this event and was discharged from the hospital the same day.
(b) (6)

Reviewer comment: FDA assessment is that the majority of both toxicity events
(hypersensitivity and IRR) were mild and did not lead to treatment discontinuation in most
cases. Product labeling provides guidance to prescribers that all patients should receive
premedication with acetaminophen, diphenhydramine, and dexamethasone prior to infusion.
Likewise, dose modification and management guidelines for infusion-related and
hypersensitivity reactions are included in Section 2 of product labeling.

Additional Safety Explorations
Human Carcinogenicity or Tumor Development
Carcinogenicity studies were not conducted with mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx or DM4.
Human Reproduction and Pregnancy
Fertility studies have not been conducted with mirvetuximab soravtansine-gynx or DM4.
Mirvetuximab soravtansine can cause embryo-fetal harm given that it contains the genotoxic
compound DM4 and it affects actively dividing cells. The pregnancy status of females of
reproductive potential should be confirmed prior to initiating therapy with mirvetuximab
soravtansine. Female patients of reproductive potential should be advised to use effective
contraception during treatment with mirvetuximab soravtansine and for 7 months after the last
dose. This information is included in Section 8 of product labeling.
Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth
Safety and effectiveness of mirvetuximab soravtansine have not been established in pediatric
patients.
Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound
Not Applicable.
Safety in the Postmarket Setting
Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience
Not Applicable.
Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting
As noted in section 8.2.5.1, FDA and the Applicant had multiple discussions regarding the
implementation of serial ophthalmologic assessments in patients treated with Mirvetuximab.
Reviewer’s Comments: FDA issued a Postmarket Requirement that the Applicant conduct a
clinical trial, or amend existing clinical trials, of mirvetuximab soravtansine to incorporate
prospectively specified, scheduled ophthalmologic assessments in all patients (symptomatic or
asymptomatic) to further characterize the incidence and severity of mirvetuximab soravtansinerelated ocular adverse events and evaluate additional risk mitigation strategies for ocular
Version date: October 12, 2018
Reference ID: 5076613

143

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761310}
{Mirvetuximab soravtansine}
adverse events. As noted in Section 8.2.7, a Postmarket Commitment was also issued to
conduct analyses in patients from racial and ethnic minority groups as well.
Integrated Assessment of Safety
FDA overall determined that Study 0417 adequately characterized the safety profile of
mirvetuximab soravtansine in the indicated population and that the pooled safety population
including additional data from Studies 0401 and 0403 were supportive of the findings on Study
0417. There were drug discontinuations due to AEs in 12% of patients in Study 0417. The main
toxicities associated with mirvetuximab soravtansine were related to gastrointestinal
symptoms, ocular adverse events and peripheral neuropathy. Ocular adverse events occurred
in the majority of patients (59%) in 0417 and this resulted in inclusion of this toxicity as a boxed
warning in the USPI proposed for the drug. FDA investigated the methods used to grade the
severity of this AE by symptoms alone and did not believe that would be adequate to monitor
ocular safety. Thus, FDA recommended routine ophthalmology examinations for the first 8
cycles of Mirvetuximab treatment due to the risk of asymptomatic toxicities progressing while
on this therapy. In addition, peripheral neuropathy adverse events were overall low grade but
affected almost one-third of patients and this toxicity was also added to the Warnings and
Precautions for mirvetuximab soravtansine though FDA agrees with the Applicant that this
patient population would be considering chemotherapy agents that also have risk for
neuropathy. Mirvetuximab soravtansine demonstrated acceptable tolerability for the indicated
population with a serious and life-threatening disease. Adverse reactions were common, but
overall low-grade and manageable with adequate safety monitoring procedures.

Statistical Issues
In general, there were no notable statistical issues with the trial design, statistical analysis plan,
or efficacy results for the Study 0417. The initial sample size included 106 patients, however,
two patients were excluded during review of the data: one for not meeting the definition of
having platinum-resistant disease and one for major protocol violation, making the Efficacy
Population inclusive of n=104 patients. The confirmed ORR was 31.7% (95% CI: 22.9, 41.6) in
the target population (n=104) with an estimated median duration of response of 5.9 months
(95% CI: 5.6, 7.7 months), per local investigator assessment (data cutoff: 11/16/21). At the 120day safety update, the median DOR by investigator assessment per RECIST v1.1 increased to 6.9
months (95% CI: 5.6, 9.7 months) (data cutoff: 4/29/22). The efficacy evaluation was based on
the magnitude of response rate and adequate duration of response.

Conclusions and Recommendations
Mirvetuximab soravtansine demonstrated an ORR of 31.7% (95% CI: 22.9, 41.6) and a median
DOR of 6.9 months (95% CI: 5.6, 9.7) in patients with FRα- positive, platinum-resistant ovarian
cancer who have received 1-3 prior systemic regimens in Study 0417. Available therapies in this
Version date: October 12, 2018
Reference ID: 5076613

144

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761310}
{Mirvetuximab soravtansine}
treatment setting include single agent chemotherapies with ORRs ranging from 6-27%,
however, these data were collected prior to establishment of a platinum-based doublet ±
bevacizumab followed by maintenance treatment with bevacizumab, niraparib, olaparib, or
olaparib + bevacizumab as 1L standard-of-care treatment for advanced ovarian cancer. The FDA
review team assessed the chemotherapy control arms from recent trials in platinum-resistant
ovarian cancer to determine an ORR of 12-13% and a median DOR of 4-7.4 months as a more
contemporary benchmark of efficacy for available therapies. There are no available therapies
specifically for treatment of FRα-positive, platinum-resistant ovarian cancer. The review team
also examined blinded ORR/DOR data from Study 0416 L, an ongoing randomized trial in a
similar treatment setting to support efficacy results from Study 0417. Considering the results
from Study 0417, supported by data from Study 0416, within the current therapeutic landscape
for platinum-resistant ovarian cancer, the review team considers that mirvetuximab
soravtansine demonstrates an improvement compared to available therapies and is reasonably
likely to predict clinical benefit in the indicated population.
Mirvetuximab soravtansine demonstrated acceptable safety and tolerability in the indicated
population with a serious and life threatening condition and limited therapeutic options. The
safety of mirvetuximab soravtansine is manageable through labeling, which includes a boxed
warning for ocular toxicity, premedication, dose modification, and supportive care. Because
mirvetuximab soravtansine was associated with high rate of ocular toxicities, a PMR will be
issued to further characterize the incidence and severity of mirvetuximab soravtansine-related
ocular adverse events and evaluate additional risk mitigation strategies for ocular adverse
events. Also, there was limited dosage exploration for mirvetuximab soravtansine, and the
product was associated with a high frequency of toxicities. It is possible that a different dosing
schedule may have a more optimal benefit-risk profile for mirvetuximab soravtansine,
therefore, FDA will issue a dose-finding postmarketing requirement for evaluation of alternate
dosing schedules.
Based on a favorable benefit-risk assessment, FDA clinical and statistical reviewers recommend
accelerated approval for the following indication:
Mirvetuximab soravtansine for the treatment of adult patients with FRα positive, platinumresistant epithelial ovarian, fallopian tube, or primary peritoneal cancer who have received one
to three prior systemic treatment regimens. Select patients for therapy based on an FDAapproved test.
This indication is approved under accelerated approval based on tumor response rate and
duration of response. Continued approval for this indication may be contingent upon verification
and description of clinical benefit in a confirmatory trial.

10 Pediatrics
Included in this BLA submission was a request for waiver for study in all pediatric age based on
the rationale that mirvetuximab soravtansine requires significant expression of FRα on the cell
membrane. The majority of FRα expression in non-epithelial pediatric cancers are cytoplasmic
in nature. Therefore, the planned waiver justification across all pediatric age groups is based on
the fact that mirvetuximab soravtansine is unlikely to provide meaningful clinical benefit to
children and is unlikely to be used.

12 Risk Evaluation and Mitigation Strategies (REMS)
Based on the benefit-risk assessment for mirvetuximab soravtansine, safety issues can be
managed through appropriate labeling and routine post-marketing surveillance. A boxed
warning for ocular toxicities has also been included in product labeling. The review team
determined that a REMS is not required for this application.

13 Postmarketing Requirements and Commitment
The following PMRs and PMCs were agreed upon in association with this accelerated approval:
1. PMR – Accelerated Approval
Conduct the clinical trial IMGN853-0416 (MIRASOL) titled “A randomized, open-label, Phase
3 study of Mirvetuximab soravtansine vs. Investigator’s choice chemotherapy in platinumresistant, advanced, high-grade epithelial ovarian, primary peritoneal, or fallopian tube
cancers with high folate receptor-alpha expression” and provide the final progression-free
survival (PFS), overall response rate (ORR) and overall survival (OS) analyses to obtain data
on the clinical efficacy of mirvetuximab soravtansine for patients with platinum-resistant
ovarian cancer.

2. PMR- FDAAA
Conduct a randomized clinical trial of at least 2 dose schedules of mirvetuximab
soravtansine to characterize the safety of the recommended dosage of 6 mg/kg AIBW Q3W
and alternative dosing schedule(s) (i.e., modified weekly dosing regimen, where lower doses
are administered at a weekly frequency, instead of every 3 weeks) for the treatment of
adult patients with FRα- positive, platinum-resistant epithelial ovarian, fallopian tube, or
primary peritoneal cancer who have received one to three prior systemic treatment
regimens. The trial should evaluate safety parameters including ocular AEs (all grade, Grade
≥ 2), peripheral neuropathy (all grade, Grade ≥ 2), and other adverse events of interest. The
trial should evaluate efficacy parameters of the alternate dosing schedules. The trial should
also include sufficient clinical pharmacokinetic sampling to analyze the exposure-response
relationship for efficacy and safety.

Soravtansine

Source: Applicant’s Population PK report QPK28-006 – Figure 16.

AIBW: adjusted ideal body weight (kg). ALB: serum albumin (g/dL). AGE: Age (y). AUCss: area
under the concentration versus time curve at steady state. Cmaxss: maximum concentration at
steady state. Dotted line represent reference (typical value of the parameter given median of
each covariate values). Grey area represent 0.8 and 1.25 fold change from the reference value.
The points and horizontal lines is the median and 95% CI and represents the ratios based on
1,000 sets of parameter estimates re-sampled from the variance covariance matrix. Covariate
effects on mirvetuximab soravtansine exposure at steady state following a 6mg/kg AIBW dose.

The FDA’s Assessment:
The Applicant’s analyses results are considered acceptable for the purpose of supporting
analyses objectives.
ER Review Issues
There are no review issues on ER.
Reviewer’s Independent Analysis

Version date: October 12, 2018
Reference ID: 5076613

193

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761310}
{Mirvetuximab soravtansine}
The Applicant’s analyses were verified by the reviewer, with no significant discordance
identified.
Overall benefit-risk evaluation based on E-R analyses
The Applicant’s Position:
The mirvetuximab soravtansine AUC0-d21 and Ctrough showed a relationship with efficacy in
terms of ORR and PFS. Mirvetuximab soravtansine AUC0-d21 was the best exposure parameter
to correlate with the adverse events of the ocular system. In general, both efficacy and ocular
toxicity were found to increase with increasing exposure to mirvetuximab soravtansine. The
covariates identified by the models overall had limited impact on efficacy and safety, and are
likely not of clinical significance.
The FDA’s Assessment:
We agree with the Applicant’s position.

Summary of Bioanalytical Method Validation and Performance
ADC (mirvetuximab soravtansine), total antibody, unconjugated DM4, and s-methyl DM4
concentrations were measured throughout the drug development program. A list of the clinical
trials and associated bioanalytical reports is provided in Table 50.

ADC (mirvetuximab soravtansine):
The bioanalytical methods for measuring mirvetuximab soravtansine plasma concentrations are
shown in Table 51 and Table 52. ELISA assays were used to measure mirvetuximab soravtansine
concentrations, which utilized a murine monoclonal antimaytansine antibody (α-maytansine)
was used for capturing mirvetuximab soravtansine, a horseradish peroxidase (HRP) conjugated
donkey antihuman immunoglobulin G (IgG) (heavy and light chain) was used for the detection,
and the 3,3′,5,5′-tetramethylbenzidine (TMB) was used as a chromogenic substrate.

Human plasma samples fortified with 1500 ng/mL IMGN853 TAb were
prepared from
10 subjects to evaluate potential matrix effects. Nine out of 10 of the
fortified samples were within 20% from the theoretical concentration.
Bias ranged from -14.7% to 21.8%.
Human plasma samples from 10 subjects were analyzed for matrix
interference. All samples were below LLOQ (<500 ng/mL).
There was no effect from hemolysis on the quantitation of IMGN853 TAb.
There was no effect from lipemia on the quantitation of IMGN853 TAb.
Dilution linearity was validated using 255000 ng/mL IMGN853 TAb as 75-,
120-, and 200-fold dilutions. The observed bias ranged from -13.1% to 9.93%.

The hook effect was evaluated by analyzing a 255000 ng/mL QC sample
undiluted and at 5-, 10-, 20-, 100-, and 250-fold dilutions. No apparent
hook effect observed at concentrations up to 255000 ng/mL.
Bench-top/process stability
Analyte stability in thawed matrix was demonstrated in sets of low- and
high-level QCs, which were thawed and remained at room temperature
for 24 hours prior to analysis.
Freeze-thaw stability
Freeze-thaw stability was demonstrated using low- and high-level QC
samples, which were frozen at -80°C ± 10°C and thawed at room
temperature during 5 cycles.

Human plasma samples fortified with DM4 or DM4-Me at 0.100 ng/mL
were prepared from 6 subjects to evaluate potential matrix effects. No
significant matrix suppression effects were indicated that could
compromise the sensitivity or accuracy of the assay.
Human plasma blanks, with and without internal standard, and low- and
high-level QC samples were fortified with 80 ng/mL pembrolizumab.
Results indicated that there is no effect on the DM4 or DM4-Me
quantitation.
There were no significant interfering peaks noted in blank human plasma
samples.
There was no effect from hemolysis on the quantification of DM4 or
DM4-Me.
There was no effect from lipemia on the quantitation of DM4 or DM4Me.

201

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Biologics License Application (BLA) 761310}
{Mirvetuximab soravtansine}
Dilution linearity and hook effect

Dilution linearity was validated using DM4 or DM4-Me at 2.40 ng/mL
diluted 2-fold and 10 ng/mL diluted 10-fold. The observed bias ranged
from -1.51% to 4.91% and
from -0.879% to 5.35% for DM4 and DM4-Me.
Bench-top/process stability
DM4 or DM4-Me analytes stability in thawed matrix was demonstrated
in sets of low- and high-level QCs (with or without 240 μg/mL IMGN853),
which were thawed and remained at room temperature for 24 hours
prior to analysis.
Freeze-thaw stability
DM4 or DM4-Me freeze-thaw stability was demonstrated using low- and
high-level QC samples, which were frozen at -70°C or -20°C and thawed
at room temperature during 5 cycles for each temperature.
Long-term storage
DM4 or DM4-Me was stable up to 1135 days at -70°C.
Carry over
Following blank sample evaluation in a sequence immediately after the
ULOQ calibration standards, there were contributions from
chromatographic peaks at the expected retention time of the analyte in
the blank samples greater than 20% of the mean analyte response for
the LLOQ calibration standards in the validation run.
